ISOLA CONTINUES ON!


============================================================================
VOLUME II — THE INNER LIVES OF ISOLA
New original companion events: emotional life, different ways of thinking, attention, sensory experiences, grief, worry, and belonging.
Creative direction: Lorsieab2 | Expansion writing: ChatGPT (OpenAI)

TRIBAL NARRATION RULES: Do not use modern diagnostic names in event prose or villagers’ dialogue. Use clear observations, not diagnoses or supernatural explanations. Do not equate differences with bad character, lack of intelligence, special powers, danger, or a need to be cured. Villagers may seek healers, trusted relatives, rest, accommodation, practical help, and community support. Not every struggle is lifelong; not every difference is an illness. Never force disclosure or public explanation. Respect privacy and autonomy. All events PG-13. These descriptions are fictional and not diagnostic checklists.

EVENT 068: The Festival That Was Too Loud
Premise: A villager who anticipated the celebration becomes distressed by overlapping drums, voices, and bright fires.
A. Offer a quieter viewing place
   Outcome: They enjoy part of the festival from a distance.
B. Ask what would help
   Outcome: They request advance notice before the loudest music.
C. Respect their wish to go home
   Outcome: They work peacefully on a carving and feel no shame about leaving.
Context and callbacks: sensory overload; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 069: The Sadness Without a Name
Premise: A gardener has lost interest in flowers, meals, and familiar games for many days.
A. Sit nearby without demanding cheer
   Outcome: The gardener appreciates quiet company, though the heaviness remains.
B. Share the garden work
   Outcome: The beds recover; the gardener still needs rest.
C. Invite a trusted healer to listen
   Outcome: The healer checks sleep, food, pain, and safety and offers ongoing care.
Context and callbacks: persistent low mood; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 070: The Worry That Would Not Leave
Premise: An apprentice repeatedly fears that the workshop fire was left burning even after checking.
A. Offer calm reassurance once
   Outcome: The apprentice settles briefly and returns to work.
B. Create a practical closing routine
   Outcome: A short checklist helps, so long as checking does not grow endless.
C. Ask how often the worry returns
   Outcome: The apprentice admits the fear consumes their evenings; a healer offers support.
Context and callbacks: intrusive worry; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 071: The Child Who Thinks Differently
Premise: A child notices tiny insect patterns but struggles when friends change the rules of a game without warning.
A. Explain the rules plainly
   Outcome: The game becomes easier for several children.
B. Invite the child to show their observations
   Outcome: Some friends become interested; others politely choose another game.
C. Offer a different activity
   Outcome: The child enjoys watching beetles with a patient friend.
Context and callbacks: social communication differences; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 072: The Thoughts That Would Not Settle
Premise: A craftsperson starts five beautiful necklaces and struggles to finish a single commission.
A. Break the work into small tasks
   Outcome: They complete one necklace and learn a useful method.
B. Allow a period for experiments
   Outcome: A surprising design emerges, but deadlines still need discussion.
C. Negotiate with customers
   Outcome: Some accept a revised schedule; others take their orders elsewhere.
Context and callbacks: attention and organization; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 073: The Child Who Could Not Sit Still
Premise: During a long lesson, a child fidgets, wanders, and misses instructions despite wanting to learn.
A. Teach while walking
   Outcome: The child remembers more while moving.
B. Give short tasks with breaks
   Outcome: The child finishes the lesson in pieces.
C. Ask what is difficult
   Outcome: The child explains the long waiting is harder than the work.
Context and callbacks: activity and attention; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 074: The Storm That Came Back
Premise: After surviving a frightening storm, a fisher startles at harmless thunder and avoids the shore.
A. Let them choose smaller steps
   Outcome: They begin with a calm morning beside a trusted companion.
B. Offer different work temporarily
   Outcome: They mend nets inland and remain useful while recovering.
C. Insist on immediate return
   Outcome: They become overwhelmed and leave; the village reconsiders its approach.
Context and callbacks: fear after danger; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 075: The Night of Little Sleep
Premise: An elder has slept poorly for weeks and grows irritable with friends.
A. Ask about discomfort and routine
   Outcome: A healer notices aching joints and helps the elder rest more comfortably.
B. Offer help with dawn chores
   Outcome: The elder accepts later mornings and feels less exhausted.
C. Dismiss the complaints
   Outcome: The elder withdraws and misses a gathering; a friend later apologizes.
Context and callbacks: sleep difficulty; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 076: The Heavy Basket
Premise: A strong worker has days of deep fatigue that others cannot see.
A. Ask what they can manage today
   Outcome: They choose a lighter task and keep their dignity.
B. Adapt the work schedule
   Outcome: Rest periods make their contributions more sustainable.
C. Accuse them of laziness
   Outcome: Trust suffers; a coworker later realizes the judgment was unfair.
Context and callbacks: fluctuating energy; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 077: The Meal Left Untouched
Premise: A villager has eaten little since a loss and insists nothing tastes good.
A. Offer a simple familiar meal
   Outcome: They eat a little with company and are not pressured to finish.
B. Ask a healer to check in
   Outcome: The healer considers illness, grief, and hydration and arranges follow-up.
C. Sit together in silence
   Outcome: The villager finds comfort even without an appetite.
Context and callbacks: grief and appetite; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 078: The Words That Stuck
Premise: A teen knows an answer but freezes when asked to speak before the entire village.
A. Let them answer privately
   Outcome: Their careful idea is heard.
B. Offer time to prepare
   Outcome: They speak a few sentences and stop when ready.
C. Invite a written or drawn answer
   Outcome: Their drawing changes the village plan.
Context and callbacks: social anxiety; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 079: The Same Path Every Morning
Premise: A villager becomes upset when an unexpected change disrupts a cherished daily routine.
A. Explain the change in advance
   Outcome: They adjust with time and help plan the new route.
B. Keep one familiar part
   Outcome: A favorite morning stop remains possible.
C. Laugh at their concern
   Outcome: The villager feels dismissed; the friend later apologizes.
Context and callbacks: routine and change; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 080: The Day of Too Many Feelings
Premise: A normally patient teen bursts into tears after several small frustrations.
A. Give them room to settle
   Outcome: They return later and explain what built up.
B. Ask whether they want company
   Outcome: A sibling sits nearby without interrogating them.
C. Demand an explanation immediately
   Outcome: The argument worsens; both later repair it.
Context and callbacks: emotional regulation; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 081: The Memory That Returned
Premise: A familiar smell brings back a frightening childhood event, leaving an adult shaken.
A. Ground them in the present
   Outcome: A trusted friend helps them notice the safe surroundings.
B. Offer a break
   Outcome: They leave the crowded workshop and return later.
C. Invite a conversation only if wanted
   Outcome: They share a little of the memory without pressure to reveal everything.
Context and callbacks: traumatic reminders; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 082: The Numbers in the Sand
Premise: A child struggles to follow spoken counting but invents an ingenious way to group shells.
A. Let them demonstrate
   Outcome: The instructor sees genuine understanding.
B. Try tangible counters
   Outcome: Several students learn more easily.
C. Ask what remains difficult
   Outcome: The child explains that long spoken instructions slip away.
Context and callbacks: learning differences; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 083: The Friend Who Needed Distance
Premise: A villager has stopped attending gatherings but continues to enjoy solitary fishing.
A. Invite without insisting
   Outcome: They decline warmly and remain content.
B. Offer quiet companionship
   Outcome: A friend joins them once a week.
C. Ask if they are lonely
   Outcome: They admit they sometimes are, and choose a small gathering.
Context and callbacks: solitude versus isolation; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 084: The Unwanted Thought
Premise: A kind caregiver is distressed by a frightening thought they never wanted and would never act on.
A. Listen without treating thought as intent
   Outcome: They feel safe enough to discuss the worry.
B. Help them speak with a trusted healer
   Outcome: The healer explains that unwanted thoughts can be upsetting and explores support.
C. Offer a calm distraction by request
   Outcome: They work together on a familiar task; the thought may still return.
Context and callbacks: intrusive thoughts without stigma; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 085: The Feast After a Loss
Premise: A parent cannot bear to attend the first harvest feast since their child died.
A. Respect their absence
   Outcome: Friends leave a meal without expecting conversation.
B. Offer a quiet remembrance
   Outcome: The parent chooses whether to participate.
C. Ask them to help prepare food
   Outcome: They accept a small task on one day and decline on another.
Context and callbacks: bereavement; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 086: The Apprentice Who Heard Too Much
Premise: After witnessing a serious accident, a young apprentice struggles to concentrate during lessons.
A. Allow a break from the worksite
   Outcome: They study a different craft for a while.
B. Ask how they are coping
   Outcome: They describe upsetting memories; a healer listens and follows up.
C. Pair them with a trusted mentor
   Outcome: They resume slowly and decide their own pace.
Context and callbacks: distress after witnessing harm; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 087: The Joy That Arrived Suddenly
Premise: A villager has an unusually energetic day, starts several grand projects, and sleeps little; friends are uncertain whether to celebrate or worry.
A. Check in without assumptions
   Outcome: The villager appreciates attention and agrees to rest.
B. Help set safe limits on commitments
   Outcome: The team postpones costly decisions until everyone has rested.
C. Consult a healer if the pattern persists
   Outcome: The healer asks about sleep, changes in behavior, and wellbeing without assigning a label.
Context and callbacks: marked shifts in energy; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 088: The Quiet Healer
Premise: A healer speaks little and dislikes crowded meetings but is attentive with patients.
A. Ask how they prefer to share findings
   Outcome: They submit concise notes.
B. Let them teach one apprentice
   Outcome: They become a thoughtful mentor.
C. Make them lead a public speech
   Outcome: They struggle and request a different way to contribute.
Context and callbacks: introversion; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 089: The Mask of Cheerfulness
Premise: A lively storyteller privately admits they feel empty despite making others laugh.
A. Believe them
   Outcome: A friend listens rather than insisting they must be happy.
B. Offer regular companionship
   Outcome: They begin taking walks together without the pressure to entertain.
C. Suggest speaking to a healer
   Outcome: The storyteller accepts help at their own pace.
Context and callbacks: hidden distress; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 090: The Fear of Leaving Home
Premise: A villager wants to visit a distant part of Isola but fears becoming trapped far from familiar places.
A. Plan a short nearby outing
   Outcome: They explore a little and choose when to return.
B. Travel with a trusted friend
   Outcome: They enjoy parts of the journey despite anxiety.
C. Postpone without shame
   Outcome: They study maps and decide whether to try another day.
Context and callbacks: avoidance and anxiety; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 091: The Argument That Grew Too Large
Premise: Two coworkers interpret the same remark differently, and one becomes overwhelmed by the rapid exchange.
A. Pause the discussion
   Outcome: Both return when calmer.
B. Put the problem in writing
   Outcome: They discover they were arguing about different tasks.
C. Ask a mediator
   Outcome: An elder helps them agree on practical boundaries.
Context and callbacks: communication differences; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 092: The Strange Quiet After Success
Premise: A researcher completes a long project but feels no triumph, only exhaustion.
A. Celebrate gently
   Outcome: They accept a small meal rather than a noisy feast.
B. Offer time away
   Outcome: Rest reveals interests they had neglected.
C. Ask what comes next
   Outcome: They admit uncertainty and choose not to rush into another project.
Context and callbacks: burnout; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 093: The Boy Who Could Not Stop Worrying
Premise: A child repeatedly asks whether their family will be safe when the rains come.
A. Explain the storm plan
   Outcome: Knowing where to shelter helps a little.
B. Practice a simple preparation task
   Outcome: The child packs supplies and feels involved.
C. Make time to listen
   Outcome: The child shares a fear the adults had not noticed.
Context and callbacks: childhood anxiety; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 094: The Apprentice Who Needed One Instruction
Premise: A trainee becomes confused when a mentor gives six directions at once.
A. Give one step at a time
   Outcome: The trainee works accurately and gains confidence.
B. Draw a sequence
   Outcome: The visual guide helps the entire workshop.
C. Ask the trainee to repeat the plan
   Outcome: They identify a confusing step before a mistake occurs.
Context and callbacks: processing and working memory; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 095: The Old Grief
Premise: An elder unexpectedly cries while repairing a toy that belonged to a deceased sibling.
A. Let the memory be spoken
   Outcome: The elder tells a funny childhood story.
B. Help finish the toy
   Outcome: It becomes a gift for a grandchild.
C. Offer privacy
   Outcome: The elder keeps the toy and the memory to themselves.
Context and callbacks: long-term grief; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 096: The Too-Perfect Pot
Premise: A potter repeatedly destroys good work because each pot has a tiny flaw.
A. Ask what matters most
   Outcome: They decide which flaws are harmless.
B. Set aside imperfect pieces
   Outcome: The pieces become practice gifts.
C. Take a rest from the wheel
   Outcome: The potter returns with less pressure, though the habit may persist.
Context and callbacks: perfectionism; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

EVENT 097: The Small Circle
Premise: A teen feels different from peers and believes everyone else has found close friends.
A. Invite them into a shared interest
   Outcome: They meet one person who enjoys the same activity.
B. Ask about their preferred company
   Outcome: They discover that one close friendship matters more to them than a crowd.
C. Respect their pace
   Outcome: They watch the festival without being pushed to socialize.
Context and callbacks: belonging; adjust by age, relationship, consent, prior events, and existing game mechanics.

CLAUDE RETROACTIVE INTEGRATION NOTE
Add these events as a companion volume to the earlier anthology. Preserve prior text and Lorsieab2 attribution. Write character-specific, age-appropriate dialogue and optional delayed callbacks. Keep modern labels out of villager-facing text, even when a real-life experience inspired an event. Do not diagnose villagers from one behavior. Include strengths, everyday flaws, humor, friendships, family, professions, and agency alongside difficulties. Avoid treating an accommodation as a cure. For severe ongoing distress, offer caring, practical and healer-led support without promises of instant recovery.

TOTAL COMPANION EVENTS: 97; NEW INNER-LIFE EVENTS: 30.
ISOLA CONTINUES ON!

THE LIVING CATALOGUE — 154 EVENTS
SOURCE FILE: Isola_The_Living_Catalogue_Magical_Fish_Plants_and_Rare_Wildlife.txt
#####################################################################################

ISOLA — THE LIVING CATALOGUE: WILD, WONDROUS & RARE
Standalone companion anthology | Original creative direction and taxonomy: Lorsieab2 | New event prose: ChatGPT

PURPOSE: Species-specific events and a catalogue that can be revised, challenged, and expanded. All events are PG-13. No worldview is made canonically correct.
FISH NAMING: FIN THEN BODY (e.g., Twin-Fin Spotanus). PLANTS: FLOWER THEN FOLIAGE (e.g., Nox Orchid).
Fish body and fin lists are combinatorial parts, not a claim that every combination was observed. Flower and foliage lists likewise. Preserve patcher-added names.
A record is an observation, not an omniscient encyclopedia. Low-skill scientists can misidentify features; high-skill ones can still be uncertain. Corrections preserve old records and credit.
A dead specimen may aid study but entails killing if captured alive; nonlethal alternatives include observation, sketch, measured descriptions, naturally deceased specimens, shed parts, fallen flowers, and cultivated offspring. Ethical decisions depend on population, ecology, consent, and purpose.
Never claim that any magical species is objectively proven supernatural; observed effects and rival explanations can coexist. Rare harvest may be prohibited, postponed, limited, or replaced with substitutes.
Villager age, ability, interests, gender and pronouns are contextual. Rare injury/death is possible but never mandatory. Missing is not dead. Skill, likes/dislikes, clothing/head changes, and delayed memories only when contextually meaningful and game-supported.

=== COMPLETE USER-SUPPLIED TAXONOMIC REFERENCE ===
FISH BODIES (28): Shark, Catfish, Arrowfish, Snout, Fatfish, Snooper, Goldshark, Fire-Arrow, Fruitfish, Flashfish, Goldbulb, Comet, Stickfish, Grouper, Leaffish, Carp, Beta, Pigmy, Bananafish, Quicksilver, Spotanus, Clownfish, Discus, Bicolor, Trigger, Surgeonfish, Tang, Idle
FISH FINS (28): Silky, Tiger, Hornet, Fanned, Pink, Snubbed, Wasp, Canary, Orange, Oriental, Golden, Crimson, Spined, Stubby, Speckled, Razorback, Greenfin, Great-Sail, Flagged, Finless, Twin-Fin, Small-Fin, Roundy, Angel, Clown, Yellow, Blue, Moorish
CREATURES (5): Golden Seahorse, Cleaning Snail, Magic Pufferfish, Blue-Spotted Stingray, Moray Eel
PLANT FOLIAGES (23): Reptans, Scandens, Grass, Orchid, Astera, Gladiatus, Fern, Ananas, Maranta, Fanleaf, Lemonbush, Bamboo, Maple, Rare Oak, Pipe Cactus, Pear Cactus, Ball Cactus, Glaber, Tigerfern, Pitcher, Weeper, Multiflora, Ridgeball
PLANT FLOWERS (37): Spotted, Painted, Daisy, Nox, Bluestar, Mystic, Rosaceae, Viola, Jalapa, Fabled, Fragrant, Blazing, Fourpetal, Aureus, Venomous, Mela, Citrus, Arthurium, Venus, Tahitian, Lilia, Baccatus, Tilia, Nightingale, Rosetta, Lilac, Veronica, Violetta, Stella, Silvermist, Anagallis, Buttercup, Cerulean, Phalaena, Mirabilis, Indigoberry, Prunus
MAGIC FISH: Magic Fish of Fertility = Greenfin Spotanus; Magic Fish of Sustenance = Speckled Leaffish; Magic Fish of Curing = Crimson Comet; Magic Fish of Growth = Oriental Goldbulb; Magic Fish of Health = Orange Snooper; Magic Fish of Mutation = Wasp Grouper; The Golden Guppy = Canary Fire-Arrow
MAGIC PLANTS: Fruit of Life = Mela Rare Oak; Flower of Knowledge = Nox Orchid; Rose of Joy = Rosaceae Fern; Golden Berries of Wealth = Aureus Scandens; Fabled Rose of Isola = Fabled Lemonbush; Magic Tree of Fragrance = Fourpetal Maple

=== EVENTS: CHOICES ARE EXCLUSIVE; EACH HAS A DISTINCT OUTCOME ===

EVENT 001 — A Record of the Shark
Premise: A young naturalist observes a Fanned Shark in a sheltered lagoon and believes its unusual feeding habits may distinguish it from other fish. Another observer disagrees about the fin shape.
  CHOICE: Sketch it from a safe distance
  OUTCOME: The drawing captures the Shark silhouette, but the fanned fin detail is disputed; an apprentice marks the page “unconfirmed.”
  CHOICE: Keep a living specimen briefly for observation
  OUTCOME: The fish reveals details missed underwater; careful husbandry and release become part of the record, though handling stresses it.
  CHOICE: Preserve a collected specimen
  OUTCOME: The record gains anatomical detail but the fish dies; villagers debate whether the knowledge justified the loss, especially if numbers are low.
  CHOICE: Wait for a second sighting
  OUTCOME: The fish disappears for days. When it returns, a different observer notes a variation that complicates the original classification.
Context / follow-up: Catalog body: Shark; observed fin: Fanned. Science skill governs errors; ethics depend on abundance; future reclassification possible.

EVENT 029 — The Silky Fin Question
Premise: A fisher claims that a Silky Arrowfish swims differently from its close relatives. A jeweler wants to use its unusual fin as a model for decorative metalwork.
  CHOICE: Study its movement in the wild
  OUTCOME: Repeated observations yield a careful description, although currents make the measurements uncertain.
  CHOICE: Sketch the fin for the jeweler
  OUTCOME: The jeweler makes a beautiful motif, but a visiting researcher points out a mistaken ray count.
  CHOICE: Examine a naturally deceased fish
  OUTCOME: The fin structure is clearer; the villager records that this specimen was found dead rather than collected alive.
  CHOICE: Capture a live fish to preserve it
  OUTCOME: A complete specimen improves comparison but kills the animal; if the population is rare, the choice prompts restrictions on future collecting.
Context / follow-up: Catalog fin: Silky; example fish: Silky Arrowfish; future comparison with other silky-finned fish.

EVENT 117 — Magic Fish of Fertility: A Question of Cause
Premise: A keeper records an unexpected increase in spawning near a Greenfin Spotanus, a fish associated in island stories with the Magic Fish of Fertility. Some call it magic, others point to water, food, chance, or observation error.
  CHOICE: Honor the traditional story
  OUTCOME: A small ceremony comforts the keeper and draws attention to the fish, without proving causation.
  CHOICE: Compare tanks and conditions
  OUTCOME: The keeper controls feeding and water changes; results are suggestive but not decisive.
  CHOICE: Ask a magician to observe
  OUTCOME: The magician reports a meaningful sensation; a skeptical apprentice records only what can be measured.
  CHOICE: Leave the fish undisturbed
  OUTCOME: The animal thrives or fades according to ordinary conditions; its mystery remains unresolved.
Context / follow-up: Exact canonical pair: Greenfin Spotanus. Magic, nature, spiritual and scientific interpretations remain viable.

EVENT 153 — The Ocean’s Library
Premise: A diver discovers a reef covered in tiny organisms whose patterns resemble writing.
  CHOICE: Sketch them underwater
  OUTCOME: The symbols prove hard to reproduce accurately.
  CHOICE: Collect a naturally detached fragment
  OUTCOME: The sample is examined without breaking the living colony.
  CHOICE: Declare them ancient script
  OUTCOME: The exciting claim spreads before evidence exists; a later correction causes embarrassment.
  CHOICE: Ask four schools of thought
  OUTCOME: Magic, nature, spirit and science each offer a reading; none becomes official truth.
Context / follow-up: Cross-reference habitat, scarcity, age, skill, belief, family and delayed outcomes; do not force a single canonical interpretation.

EVENT 154 — The Keeper of Unfinished Records
Premise: An elderly scientist has hundreds of sketches with uncertain names and fears dying before completing the catalogue.
  CHOICE: Train apprentices
  OUTCOME: The records become a multigenerational project.
  CHOICE: Publish with uncertainty labels
  OUTCOME: Other scholars can correct mistakes without erasing the elder’s work.
  CHOICE: Focus on one beloved species
  OUTCOME: The elder completes a careful monograph and finds peace with what remains unknown.
  CHOICE: Keep everything private
  OUTCOME: The collection survives only if family members later discover it.
Context / follow-up: Cross-reference habitat, scarcity, age, skill, belief, family and delayed outcomes; do not force a single canonical interpretation.

=== IMPLEMENTATION PROMPT FOR CLAUDE ===
Retain every event and all supplied exact names. This is a separate new companion anthology; do not overwrite earlier collections. Build searchable catalogue entries for every supplied fish body, fin, plant foliage, flower, magical fish, magical plant and creature. Do not falsely assert that every combinatorial hybrid has been encountered. Generate valid names FIN + BODY and FLOWER + FOLIAGE. Keep observed names, suspected names and corrected names separate; let scientist skill and conditions affect accuracy. Add habitat, date/season, abundance estimate, conservation status, provenance, observer, sketch quality, live observation, specimen method and ethics notes. Record whether collection required death, whether an animal was already dead, or whether nonlethal alternatives were used. Add follow-up events for endangered species and discoveries that might outperform common materials or treatments, but include ecological cost, uncertainty, substitutes, propagation, and failed experiments. Preserve ambiguity around magical efficacy. Respect PG-13 and user canon.
